We end this section by providing a proof of the

HOLGRIM Convergence Theorem

10.1
.
Our strategy is to use the separation of the points
selected during the
HOLGRIM
algorithm, established in
the
Point Separation Lemma

9.1
, to obtain
the desired upper bound on the number of steps which the
algorithm can completed before terminating.

Proof of Theorem

10.1
.

Let
{\cal N},\Lambda,c,d\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
\gamma>0
with

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
such that
\gamma\in(k,k+1]
, and
fix a choice of
q\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
.
Let
\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}
be real numbers such that

0\leq\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{\varepsilon}{cd^{q}}
.
Let
V
and
W
be finite dimensional real Banach
spaces, of dimensions
d
and
c
respectively,
with
\Sigma\subset V
finite.
Assume that the tensor products of
V
are all
equipped with admissible norms (cf. Definition

2.1
).
Assume, for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}
, that

f_{i}=\left(f_{i}^{(0)},\ldots,f_{i}^{(k)}\right)\in{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)
is
non-zero and define

{\cal F}:=\left\{f_{i}~:~i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}\right\}\subset{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)
.
Choose scalars
A_{1},\ldots,A_{{\cal N}}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such
that, for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}
, we have

||f_{i}||_{{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)}\leq A_{i}
.
Given
a_{1},\ldots,a_{{\cal N}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{0\}
,
define
\varphi=\left(\varphi^{(0)},\ldots,\varphi^{(k)}\right)\in\Span({\cal F})\subset{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)

and constants
C,D>0
by
(cf.
(
10.1
)
)

({\bf I})\quad\varphi:=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}a_{i}f_{i},\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi^{(l)}:=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}a_{i}f^{(l)}_{i}\\
\text{for every }l\in\{0,\ldots,k\}\end{array}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad({\bf II})\quad C:=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}|a_{i}|A_{i}>0.

(10.65)

and (cf.
(
10.2
)
)

D=D(d,k):=\sum_{s=0}^{k}\beta(d,s)={{\sum}}_{s=0}^{k}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d+s-1\\
s\end{array}\right).

(10.66)

With a view to later applying the
HOLGRIM
algorithm
to approximate
\varphi
on
\Sigma
, for each

i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}
let
\tilde{a}_{i}:=|a_{i}|

and
\tilde{f}_{i}
be given by
f_{i}
if
a_{i}>0
and

-f_{i}
if
a_{i}<0
.
Observe that for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}

we have that
\left|\left|\tilde{f}_{i}\right|\right|_{{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)}=||f_{i}||_{{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)}
. Moreover,
we also have that

\tilde{a}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{a}_{{\cal N}}>0
and that

\varphi=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\tilde{a}_{i}\tilde{f}_{i}
.
Further, for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}
we set

h_{i}:=\tilde{f}_{i}/A_{i}
and

\alpha_{i}:=\tilde{a}_{i}A_{i}
(cf.
HOLGRIM

(B)
).
A first consequence is that for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}

we have that
||h_{i}||_{{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)}\leq 1
.
Further consequences are that
C
satisfies

C=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\left|a_{i}\right|A_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\tilde{a}_{i}A_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\alpha_{i},

(10.67)

and, for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}
, that

\alpha_{i}h_{i}=\tilde{a}_{i}\tilde{f}_{i}=a_{i}f_{i}
.
Thus the expansion for
\varphi
in (
I
) of

(
10.65
)
is equivalent to

\varphi=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\alpha_{i}h_{i},\qquad\text{and hence}\qquad||\varphi||_{{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\alpha_{i}||h_{i}||_{{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\alpha_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}({\ref{eq:HOLGRIM_conv_thm_pf_new_C}})}}}{{=}}C.

(10.68)

Define a positive constant

r=r(C,\gamma,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0},q)>0

as in
(
10.3
)
. That is,

r:=\sup\left\{\lambda>0~:~2C\lambda^{\gamma-q}+\varepsilon_{0}e^{\lambda}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{cd^{q}}\right\}.

(10.69)

Define

N=N(\Sigma,C,\gamma,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0},q)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
to be the
r
-packing
number of
\Sigma
as in

(
10.4
)
.
That is,

N:=\max\left\{s\in{\mathbb{Z}}~:~\exists~z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}\in\Sigma\text{ for which }||z_{a}-z_{b}||_{V}>r\text{ if }a\neq b\right\},

(10.70)

Consider applying the
HOLGRIM

algorithm to approximate
\varphi
on
\Sigma
, with

M:=\min\left\{\frac{{\cal N}-1}{cD},\Lambda\right\}
,
the target accuracy in
HOLGRIM

(A)

is
\varepsilon
,
the acceptable recombination error in

HOLGRIM

(A)
is
\varepsilon_{0}
,
the order level in
HOLGRIM

(A)
is
q
,
the shuffle numbers in
HOLGRIM

(A)

are
s_{1}=\ldots=s_{M}=1
, the integers

k_{1},\ldots,k_{M}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
in

HOLGRIM

(A)
are

k_{1}=\ldots=k_{M}=1
, and the scaling factors in

HOLGRIM

(A)
as

A_{1},\ldots,A_{{\cal N}}>0
.
Suppose that the integer
N
in

(
10.4
)
satisfies

N\leq M
.

We first prove that the algorithm terminates after completing,
at most,
N
steps.
If
N=M
then this is immediate since the
HOLGRIM

algorithm is guaranteed to terminate after step
M
.
Hence we only verify the claim in the non-trivial setting that

N<M
, in which case terminating after (at most)
N
steps
requires the termination criterion in
HOLGRIM

(D)
to be triggered.

Let
z_{1}\in\Sigma
be the point chosen in the first step
(cf.
HOLGRIM

(C)
).
Let
u_{1}\in\Span({\cal F})
be the approximation found via
the
HOLGRIM Recombination Step
satisfying, in
particular, that
\Lambda^{k}_{\varphi-u_{1}}(z_{1})\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.
Define
\Sigma_{1}:=\{z_{1}\}\subset\Sigma
.
We conclude via
(
9.3
)

in Lemma

9.1
that, for
every
z\in{\overline{\mathbb{B}}}_{V}(z_{1},r)\cap\Sigma
, we have

\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u_{1}}(z)\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
.

If
N=1
then
(
10.70
)
means that

{\overline{\mathbb{B}}}_{V}(z_{1},r)\cap\Sigma=\Sigma
.
Thus we have established, for every
z\in\Sigma
, that

\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u_{1}}(z)\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
.
For each
z\in\Sigma
it follows from

(
5.62
)
in Lemma

5.5

that, for every
\sigma\in{\cal T}_{z,q}
, we have

|\sigma(\varphi-u_{1})|\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
.
Recalling
HOLGRIM

(D)
, this means
that the algorithm terminates before step
2
is completed.
Hence the algorithm terminates after completing

N=1
steps.

If
N\geq 2
then we note that if the stopping criterion in

HOLGRIM

(D)
is triggered at the start
of step
l\in\{2,\ldots,N\}
then we evidently have that
the algorithm has terminated after carrying out no more than

N
steps.
Consequently, we need only deal with the case in which the
algorithm reaches and carries out step
N
without terminating.
In this case, we claim that the algorithm terminates after
completing step
N
, i.e. that the termination criterion at
the start of step
N+1
is triggered.

Before proving this we fix some notation.
Recalling
HOLGRIM

(D)
,
for
l\in\{2,\ldots,N\}
let
z_{l}\in\Sigma
denote
the point selected at step
l
, and let
u_{l}\in\Span({\cal F})

denote the approximation found via the

HOLGRIM Recombination Step
satisfying, for
every
s\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
, that

\Lambda^{k}_{\varphi-u_{l}}(z_{s})\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.

By appealing to Lemma
9.1

we deduce both that, whenever
s,t\in\{1,\ldots,l\}

with
s\neq t
, we have
(cf.
(
9.4
)
)

||z_{t}-z_{s}||_{V}>r,

(10.71)

and that whenever
t\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
and

z\in{\overline{\mathbb{B}}}_{V}(z_{t},r)\cap\Sigma
we have
(cf.
(
9.3
)
)

\Lambda^{b}_{\varphi-u_{l}}(z)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{cd^{q}}.

(10.72)

Consider step
N+1
of the algorithm at which we
examine the quantity

K:=\max\left\{|\sigma(\varphi-u_{N})|:\sigma\in\Sigma^{\ast}_{q}\right\}
.
If
K\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
then the algorithm terminates without
carrying out step
N+1
, and thus has terminated after carrying
out
N
steps.

Assume that
K>\varepsilon/cd^{q}
so that

\sigma_{N+1}:=\argmax\left\{|\sigma(\varphi-u_{N})|:\sigma\in\Sigma^{\ast}_{q}\right\}
satisfies

|\sigma_{N+1}(\varphi-u_{N})|>\frac{\varepsilon}{cd^{q}}.

(10.73)

Recall from the
HOLGRIM Extension Step
that

z_{N+1}\in\Sigma
is taken to be the point for which

\sigma_{N+1}\in{\cal T}_{z_{N+1},q}
.
It is then a consequence of Lemma
5.3

that (cf.
(
5.33
)
)

\left|\sigma_{N+1}\left(\varphi-u_{N}\right)\right|\leq\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u_{N}}(z_{N+1}).

(10.74)

Thus we have that

\frac{\varepsilon}{cd^{q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}({\ref{eq:HOLGRIM_conv_thm_pf_N+1_lin_func_lb}})}}}{{<}}\left|\sigma_{N+1}(\varphi-u_{N})\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}({\ref{eq:HOLGRIM_conv_thm_pf_sigm_j_eval_ub}})}}}{{\leq}}\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u_{N}}(z_{N+1}).

(10.75)

Together,
(
10.72
)

for
l:=N
and

(
10.75
)

yield that, for every
t\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, we
have
z_{N+1}\notin{\overline{\mathbb{B}}}_{V}(z_{t},r)
; that is,

||z_{N+1}-z_{t}||_{V}>r
.

Therefore the points
z_{1},\ldots,z_{N+1}\in\Sigma

satisfy that, whenever
s,t\in\{1,\ldots,N+1\}

with
s\neq t
, we have
||z_{s}-z_{t}||_{V}>r
.
Hence

N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}({\ref{eq:HOLGRIM_conv_thm_pf_N}})}}}{{=}}\max\left\{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}:\exists~x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\in\Sigma\text{ such that }||x_{s}-x_{t}||_{V}>r\text{ whenever }s\neq t\right\}\geq N+1

(10.76)

which is evidently a contradiction.
Thus we must in fact have that
K\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
,
and hence the algorithm must terminate before carrying out
step
N+1
.

Having established the claimed upper bound for the maximum
number of steps that the
HOLGRIM
algorithm
can complete without terminating, we turn our attention
to establishing the properties claimed for the approximation
returned after the algorithm terminates.
Let
m\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
be the integer for which the

HOLGRIM
algorithm terminates after step
m
.
Let
u:=u_{m}\in\Span({\cal F})
.
Then
u
is the final approximation returned by the

HOLGRIM
algorithm.

If
m<\{1,\ldots,M-1\}
then the termination
criterion of
HOLGRIM

(D)
is triggered
at the start of step
m+1
.
Consequently, we have that for every linear functional

\sigma\in\Sigma_{q}^{\ast}
that

|\sigma(\varphi-u)|\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
.
Recall that
\Sigma^{\ast}_{q}:=\cup_{z\in\Sigma}{\cal T}_{z,q}
.
Thus, for any
z\in\Sigma
, we must have
that
|\sigma(\varphi-u)|\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
for
every
\sigma\in{\cal T}_{z,q}
.
Consequently, by appealing to Lemma

5.5

(specifically to the implication

(
5.63
)
),
we conclude that
\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u}(z)\leq\varepsilon
.
Since
z\in\Sigma
was arbitrary we have established
that
\max_{z\in\Sigma}\left\{\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u}(z)\right\}\leq\varepsilon
.

If
m=M
then, since
N\leq M
and
we have already established that the

HOLGRIM
terminates before completing step
k
for
any
k>N
, we must have that
N=M
.
Consequently, in this case we have that
u=u_{N}
, and thus
have previously established that for every linear functional

\sigma\in\Sigma_{q}^{\ast}
we have

|\sigma(\varphi-u)|\leq\varepsilon/cd^{q}
.
And so, by repeating the argument of the paragraph above,
we may once again conclude that

\max_{z\in\Sigma}\left\{\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u}(z)\right\}\leq\varepsilon
.

The combination of the previous two paragraphs enables us
to conclude that

\max_{z\in\Sigma}\left\{\Lambda^{q}_{\varphi-u}(z)\right\}\leq\varepsilon

as claimed in the second part of

(
10.6
)
.

Recalling
HOLGRIM

(C)
and
(D)
,
the
HOLGRIM Recombination Step
has found

u_{m}\in\Span({\cal F})
satisfying, for every

s\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
that
\Lambda^{k}_{\varphi-u_{m}}(z_{s})\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.
In particular, this is achieved via an application of Lemma 3.1
in
[
LM22-I
]
to a system of
Q_{m}:=1+mcD(d,k)

real-valued equations
(cf.
(
6.5
)
and

HOLGRIM Recombination Step

(C)
).
Therefore recombination returns non-negative coefficients

b_{1},\ldots,b_{Q_{m}}\geq 0
with

\sum_{s=1}^{Q_{m}}b_{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{{\cal N}}\alpha_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}({\ref{eq:HOLGRIM_conv_thm_pf_new_C}})}}}{{=}}C,

(10.77)

and indices

e(1),\ldots,e(Q_{m})\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}
for which

u=\sum_{s=1}^{Q_{m}}b_{s}h_{e(s)}\quad\text{so that for every }j\in\{0,\ldots,n\}\quad u^{(j)}=\sum_{s=1}^{Q_{m}}b_{s}h^{(j)}_{e(s)}.

(10.78)

A consequence of
(
10.78
)

is that

u=\sum_{s=1}^{Q_{m}}b_{s}h_{e(s)}=\sum_{s=1}^{Q_{m}}\frac{b_{s}}{A_{e(s)}}\tilde{f}_{e(s)}.

(10.79)

For each
s\in\{1,\ldots,Q_{m}\}
,
define
c_{s}:=\frac{b_{s}}{A_{e(s)}}
if

\tilde{f}_{e(s)}=f_{e(s)}
(which, we recall, is
the case if
a_{e(s)}>0
) and

c_{s}:=-\frac{b_{s}}{A_{e(s)}}
if

\tilde{f}_{e(s)}=-f_{e(s)}
(which, we recall, is
the case if
a_{e(s)}<0
).
Then
(
10.79
)

gives the expansion for

u\in{\mathrm{Lip}}(\gamma,\Sigma,W)
in terms of
the functions
f_{1},\ldots,f_{{\cal N}}

claimed in the first part of

(
10.6
)
.
Moreover, from
(
10.77
)

we have that

\sum_{s=1}^{Q_{m}}\left|c_{s}\right|A_{e(s)}=\sum_{s=1}^{Q_{m}}b_{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}({\ref{eq:HOLGRIM_conv_thm_pf_approx_coeff_sum}})}}}{{=}}C

(10.80)

as claimed in
(
10.5
)
.

It remains only to prove that if the coefficients

a_{1},\ldots,a_{{\cal N}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{0\}

corresponding to
\varphi

(cf. (
I
) of
(
10.65
)
)
are all positive (i.e.
a_{1},\ldots,a_{{\cal N}}>0
) then
the coefficients
c_{1},\ldots,c_{Q_{m}}\in{\mathbb{R}}

corresponding to
u

(cf.
(
10.79
)
) are
all non-negative (i.e.
c_{1},\ldots,c_{Q_{m}}\geq 0
).
First note that
a_{1},\ldots,a_{{\cal N}}>0
means, for
every
i\in\{1,\ldots,{\cal N}\}
, that

\tilde{f}_{i}=f_{i}
. Consequently, for every

s\in\{1,\ldots,Q_{m}\}
, we have that

\tilde{f}_{e(s)}=f_{e(s)}
, and so by definition
c_{s}=b_{s}/A_{e(s)}
.
Since
A_{e(s)}>0
and
b_{s}\geq 0
, it follows that
c_{s}\geq 0
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
10.1
.
∎
